WARRINGTON OUTPLAY RUNCORN.

THE ASSOCIATION GAME.

gaine and the homesters were only,: allowed to pay brief visits to their territory. During an attack on the home goal the visitors were awarded a penalty, from which they opened their account. Following this there was a period of even play, after which the visitors again attacked, and, following some brisk work, registered their second goal. Encouraged by these successes they continued to hold the upper hand and managed to steer the ball past the home goalkeeper a third time. The homesters, rousing themselves, made a determined attack on their opponents' citadel and were sucdessful in reducing the arrears. At half-time the score was 3 goals to 1 in favour of the visitors. On the resumption the home team played more strenuously with the breeze in their favour and had a greater share of the game. During an attack on the visitors' goal one of the defenders scooped the ball away With his hand, and, from the ensuing free-kick, the home team registered their second goal. Heartened by this success, the homesters were all over their opponents, penning them in their own half, and from an accurate centre from the right the scores were made level. Both teams struggled desperately for the lead, some keen play being witnessed. Both ends were visited, and towards the end the home team were successful in scoring again. Result : Longford, 4 goals; Earlestown Mission, 3. ELMWOOD CONS. v. EMMANUEL INSTITUTE. On Saturday the Cons., having no League match on band, met the Institute in a friendly encounter on their ground in Manchester-road. The following players turned out:— Elmwood Cons.: James Phcenix ; Joe Phcenix and W. Hall; Pritchard, E. B. Mellor and J. Mellor; Smith, Gleave, Ashton, Wells and Lamb. Institute: Bailey; S. Hughes and Crozier; Oakes, Banner and Eyre; Burgess, L. Hughes, Roberts, Eaton and Bevan. The home team won the toss and defended the Cemetery goal. The game opened very evenly, but gradually the Cons. gained ground in the visitors' half and Bailey had to concede a corner to clear. The flag-kick proved fruit- less, and the Institute rushed to the home end, where mis-kicking by the backs let them in, but Phcenix put the ball over the bar. The corner-kick did not benefit the visitors, and the ' transferred‘ play to the other end, where they gained a penalty kick. Lamb took the kick and opened the scoring. This reverse roused the visitors and the home defence was hard pressed. Roberts missed a very easy chance by shooting over the bar, and Plicenix did likewise at the opposite end. From a corner-kick, well placed by Eaton, Bevan made the scores equal, but directly afterwards Roberts regained the lead for the Institute, the home backs being at fault The Institute were leading at half-time by 2 goals to 1. On resuming, the home team quickly attacked the visitors' goal, and D. Gleave equalised the score. The home team showed up to better ad- vantage, but offside spoilt their efforts. After Lamb had been penalised for, a foul, he shot wide when he had a good opening. The Insti- tute began to have more of the game and some exciting incidents were seen. Ashton was penal- ised inside the area and Crozier took the kick, but Phcenix made a good save. After the visitors had missed several good openings th: home forwards transferred play to the opposite, goal, where Ashton gained the lead. From the place-kick both ends were visited in turn, but no further goals were scored, and at the call of time the score was :—Elmwood Corp., 3 goals; Emmanuel Institute, 2 goals. ITEMS. St. Barnabas' turned up at Newton with de- pleted ranks, but some of their supporters filled up the gaps very satisfactorily. Hinds, in goal, proved himself a very useful emergency man. The half-back play of the Cons. and St. Bar- rabas' was very good. Glover and Long proved a bit better than Lawrence and Tither, in the respective back divisions. Dodd, Rhubotham and Smart proved a very good inside trio for the Cons., but they were at fault in finessing when they ought to have shot. Fairfield United met with their first reverse at the hands (or, rather, feet) of the White Star. The Star are to be congratulated on their success. The United have a strong half-back line in Ditchfield, Hunter and Payne. Meachin was in opposition to his old club- mate, Millward. • Stanley Woodhouse did not get much chance to display his quality. The Orford White Star are shining well at present. Their half-back line—Caldwell, Denny and Gifford—has a Latchford flavour about it. , The Central v. Baptists game was poor in the first half, but in the second portion the Central improved greatly and the Baptists were easily defeated. Bank Quay Recs. and Paddington divided the points. George, the Paddington goalkeeper, was in his best form. Holmes and Spencer played with good under- standing. Craik and S. Smith were the most dangerous of the visitors' forwards. Kendrick and Davies were most prominent for the home forwards. There was a good slice of luck about the shot that equalised. Elmwood Cons. registered their second victory of the season against Emmanuel Institute, this being the latter's first defeat this year. James Pluenix gave a good display in the home goal. J. Brown and J. Chamberlain shaped well on their first appearance with Paddington United. W. Robinson (Latchford), T. Woodhead (Stockton Heath), and H. Wainwright (Em- manuel Old Boys) have been secured by Pad- dington, and make their first appearance to-day in the Reserve team. Fairfield, what of the Star half-back line? Caldwell, the Star "skipper," is indeed bringing his team out to championship form. FIXTURES WANTED. Emmanuel Institute have several open dates, which they would like to fill in with any friendly team. Secretaries must apply to:— A. Oakes, lion. secretary, 136, Knutsford-road, Latchford, Warrington.
